## Context

- A monomorphic property load must be one shape guard and one load (02 §6 H17). So the shape must say where each field is, and the object must not need a second lookup.
- Depends on H8 (word0, ShapeId = offset in the shape space), H9/H11 (barrier v2 for word0), H18 (elements), H19 (atom keys). H16 (ICs) and the JIT/AOT tiers depend on it.

## Decision

v0 (M0 wk4) builds the plan's JSObject header with shapes in the shape space. The plan items marked "decide before M1a" below are not built.

```text
 +0  word0   shape_id u32 | gc_state | kind | flags u16 (bits 0-7 in-object slot count, bit 8 is-prototype)
 +8  SLOTS (fast, out-of-object fields) | PROPS (dictionary table) | undefined
 +16 ELEMENTS | undefined
 +24 fixed fields of the kind, then in-object slots [capacity]

 shape (64 B, Space::Shape):  +8 proto  +16 parent  +24 descriptors  +32 transitions
                              +40 transition table | PROTO_INFO (unique)  +48 counts  +56 flags
```

| Kind | Fixed fields | Default capacity |
|---|---|---|
| OBJECT | none | 4 for `{}`; n for a literal with n named keys; 8 for `new F` and other prototypes |
| FUNCTION | +24 info, +32 context, +40 home, +48 realm (a REALM cell; OBJECT_LAYOUT 3) | 2 (`length`, `name`), at +56 |
| ARRAY | +24 length | 0 |
| ERROR | +24 unit, +32 offset | 2 |
| ITERATOR | +24 iterated (array iterator: an object, null while its `next` runs user code, undefined when done; string iterator: a string, "" when done), +32 next, +40 kind (values 0, keys 1, entries 2; OBJECT_LAYOUT 2) | 0 |
| PRIMITIVE_WRAPPER | +24 value | 0 |
| BOUND_FUNCTION | +24 target, +32 this, +40 args | 2 |
| GENERATOR (OBJECT_LAYOUT 2) | +24 state, +32 mode, +40 function, +48 receiver, +56 context, +64 registers (an ELEMENTS cell), +72 resume mode, +80 async slot | 0 |
| SLOT_OBJECT (OBJECT_LAYOUT 2) | +24 ObjectClass id, +32 internal slots (an ELEMENTS cell of n Values, or undefined when n = 0); property code never touches fixed fields | 0 |
| ARGUMENTS (25, OBJECT_LAYOUT 3) | +24 parameter map (a CONTEXT, or undefined for an unmapped object), +32 mapped count (int32); the elements at +16 | 3 (`length`, @@iterator, `callee`) |
| MODULE_NAMESPACE (26, OBJECT_LAYOUT 3) | +24 module (a MODULE cell), +32 export names (an ELEMENTS cell of sorted strings) | 1 (@@toStringTag) |
| PROXY (27, OBJECT_LAYOUT 4) | a Proxy exotic object (§10.5) whose target is not callable: `[[ProxyTarget]]` and `[[ProxyHandler]]` in fixed fields (null once revoked); M4-P4 PROXY1 sets the offsets and registers the kind | set by PROXY1 |
| PROXY_CALLABLE (28, OBJECT_LAYOUT 4) | the same, for a callable target; a separate kind so that IsCallable stays a kind test (`kinds::is_callable`; V9-D19) | set by PROXY1 |

| Internal cell (OBJECT_LAYOUT 3) | Fields (from +8) | Size |
|---|---|---|
| MODULE (76) | the Source Text Module Record (§16.2.1.4-6): status, environment, namespace, unit (native word), requested modules, loaded modules, evaluation error (hole = none), DFS index, DFS ancestor index, cycle root, has-TLA, async evaluation order, top-level capability, async parent modules, pending async dependencies, host-defined | 136 B |
| ORDERED_TABLE (77) | +8 capacity, +16 live count, +24 deleted count, +32 bucket count (a power of 2), +40 entry width (3 for Map, 2 for Set), +48 flags (bit 0 = weak keys, set at creation, never changed); then the bucket heads and the entries. Kind and trace function in `ordered_table.rs` | 56 + 8 x (buckets + capacity x width) |
| REALM (78) | +8 global object, +16 global lexical record, +24 intrinsics (an ELEMENTS cell, index = `IntrinsicId`), +32 template registry, +40 host-defined. One per isolate in M3-P0 (the `Root::Realm` root, `Cx.realm`) | 48 B |

| Cell (OBJECT_LAYOUT 4, M4-P0; declared, built by the named role) | Fields (from +8) | Builder |
|---|---|---|
| CONS (3, a string) | +8 length (u32), +12 hash field (u32) (the string header, `string.rs`), +16 first, +24 second | M4-P1 STR1 |
| SLICED (4, a string) | +8 length (u32), +12 hash field (u32) (the string header), +16 parent (a flat string), +24 offset (int32) | M4-P1 STR1 |
| THIN (5, a string) | +8 length (u32), +12 hash field (u32) (the string header), +16 actual (the atom it forwards to), +24 int32 0 ([2026-11-01] since M4-P2 STR2, b26be980: a THIN cell keeps the 32 B of the CONS or SLICED cell it was) | M4-P2 STR2 |
| VALIDITY_CELL (79) | a prototype's validity cell (V9-D7): a valid bit and a weak users list; PROTO_INFO +32 holds it (was reserved; traced strongly) | M4-P1 VALIDITY |
| WEAK_CELL (80) | the weak target of a WeakRef or of a FinalizationRegistry entry (V9-D23); WeakRef and FinalizationRegistry are SLOT_OBJECT classes 11 and 12 | M4-P4 WEAKREF |

These are the whole month-4 kind list (plan v9 rule 18): construct sites and instanceof caches are IC_HANDLER cells with new handler kinds (IC_LAYOUT 2, H16), and a literal site keeps only its feedback slot halves. M4-P0 registers none of these kinds; the role that first allocates one registers its trace function (`tests::layout4::every_month_4_kind_is_registered`, since M4-P4). [2026-10-29, C-24 (V10-D5, decided M5-P0; text M5-P3 M1A-CODE):] **The kind ids are an append-only list.** No id is renumbered or reused, and a retired id stays reserved; a new kind takes a new id in its range of the `kinds.rs` table (the `kinds.rs` module doc holds the same rule). `kinds::is_callable` stays three compares (adjacent callable ids would need a renumbering), and `exotic::is_routed` stays the one range test 25..=28, a closed range: a new kind with routed internal methods gets its own compare. The list's field is the kind byte (word0 byte 5): 255 ids, 0 refused, no wrap. 255 is never assigned: negative tests probe it, never "the next free id". Checks: `tests::layout3::kind_ids_are_an_append_only_byte_list` (the ids as built, each in its range, the routed range exactly ARGUMENTS, MODULE_NAMESPACE, PROXY, PROXY_CALLABLE of all 256 bytes), `a_cell_of_a_never_assigned_kind_passes_no_kind_test`, `register_kinds_registers_the_five_new_kinds` (probe 255); `kinds::tests::table_v1_ranges`.

Every word after word0 is a Value (a native pointer uses the native-word encoding), so each new kind has one trace function over its whole size. The exotic internal methods of ARGUMENTS and MODULE_NAMESPACE are routed from `exotic.rs` and the `property.rs` set, define, delete, own-keys, array-index and for-in paths to `arguments.rs` and `module.rs` (one kind test per hook); a routed object on a prototype chain decides the `[[Set]]` walk through its own `[[Set]]` (§10.1.9.2 step 1.b), and the `[[Get]]` and `[[GetOwnProperty]]` hooks may throw (a namespace binding in its TDZ). The M3-P0 stubs take the ordinary path.

| Item | v0 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Shape of an object | `(o & !(CAGE_ALIGN - 1)) \| word0.shape_id`: no heap pointer needed. `ep9_vm` asks for a 64 GiB aligned cage of at most 64 GiB (`shape::heap_config`) | `shape::shape_of`, decision W4-OBJ-CAGE |
| Field i | `i < capacity`: in-object slot i; else `SLOTS[i - capacity]` (grows to max(need, 2 x old, 4)) | `props::field_ref` |
| Fast shapes | Transition tree; one descriptor array shared along a chain (prefix rule); hash index above 8 keys; weak transitions | `shape.rs` (P1-SHAPE) |
| Dictionary mode (OM-10) | Delete that is not the last; attribute change (data <-> accessor too); array-index key on a non-array (since M5-P1 ELEM1, H18-Q3: only an index key that the elements path does not accept. The path accepts a new {w, e, c} data element of a kind with a +16 elements store (`elements::plain_elements_kind`), as an int32 key, or as a string index key once the object has an elements cell, if `object::element_set` takes the index (less than `elements::PLAIN_GAP`, 1024, past the capacity and below `object::arr::MAX_LENGTH`); such a key goes to the +16 elements store. The other index keys stay stored properties and still make a dictionary: a string index key on an object with no elements cell; an index that `object::element_set` refuses (`o[0] = 1` then `o['100000'] = 2`); a [[Set]] of a new index on an object with no elements cell once a prototype has had an index key (`shape::proto_index_keys`: `Object.prototype[9] = 'p'` then `q = {}; q[0] = 1`); an accessor or other attributes; and an index key on a PRIMITIVE_WRAPPER (the routed kinds take their own hooks)); SetPrototypeOf with own properties; PreventExtensions; 1020 properties; 64 live transitions. One shared dictionary shape per (prototype, kind, extensible); a prototype object gets a unique one | `props::to_dictionary` |
| Prototype mode | When an object becomes a `[[Prototype]]` on any path (a new object, SetPrototypeOf of any object, a prototype included): is-prototype flag bit, a unique shape (no parent, no transitions) whose +40 holds the object's PROTO_INFO (its root and dictionary shapes). So every shape's proto is null or a prototype-mode object | `shape::proto_info_for`, `shape::with_proto` |
| Epoch (OM-8) | Any structural change of a prototype bumps `ShapeState::proto_epoch`: a named property or an array element that appears or goes away (a `length` cut that removes an element too), an attribute change, SetPrototypeOf, PreventExtensions, a dictionary move. A value store does not (a named or element value, `length` growth, a `length` cut over holes only). Only a prototype pays more than a flag test. The epoch saturates at `u32::MAX` and never wraps (W4-P3FIX-EPOCH-SAT; H16 K18) | `shape::note_change`, `shape::EPOCH_SATURATED`; elements: `object::array_set`, `object::array_set_length` (the only element writers) |
| Index keys on prototypes | A sticky per-runtime flag (`shape::proto_index_keys`) is set when a prototype-mode object gets an array-index own property (any attributes), or when an object that may have one becomes a prototype. While it is clear, `[[Set]]` and `Array.prototype.push` add a missing array element with no prototype walk; once set, they walk the chain (§10.1.9.2). It is never cleared, like V8's protector (W4-P3FIX-ELEM-FLAG, -ANY-INDEX, -PROTO-SCAN, -PUSH) | `shape::note_proto_index_key`, `shape::proto_info_for`, `property::array_store` |
| Extensible, `[[Prototype]]` | In the shape (flag EXTENSIBLE, +8 proto). A fast shape is always extensible | `property::prevent_extensions` |
| Keys | Stored keys are atoms or symbols (`key::intern_key` on every define path); a lookup classifies its probe once | W4-KEY-EQ |
| Accessors | Attribute bit 8 (`attr::ACCESSOR`) in descriptors and dictionary entries; the value slot holds an immutable ACCESSOR_PAIR (see "Accessors" below) | `accessor.rs`, `object::attr` |
| Functions | `length`, `name` are own data properties {w: false, e: false, c: true} in the 2 slots. `prototype` is made on first use, and before any later key is added, so the key order is right | `object::new_function`, `exotic.rs` |

### Accessors (M0 wk4, P3-ACC)

JSC style (plan §2 row 6): an accessor is an attribute bit plus an immutable pair cell in the
ordinary value slot, so a shape describes an accessor exactly as it describes a data field.

```text
 descriptor details: attrs = ACCESSOR | e | c (never WRITABLE), field i
 field i (in-object, SLOTS or PROPS entry) ──> ACCESSOR_PAIR (kind 67, 24 B): +8 getter, +16 setter
                                               (a callable, or undefined when absent)
```

| Item | v0 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Pair | Immutable. `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` builds a new pair from the descriptor and the old halves (6.c keeps a half the descriptor does not name) and stores it in place of the old one | `accessor::new_pair`, `property::define_own_property` |
| `[[Get]]` | The first property on the chain decides; an accessor calls its getter with the receiver as `this` (a primitive base passes itself), undefined without a getter | `property::get_with_receiver`, `accessor::call_getter` |
| `[[Set]]` | The first property on the chain decides; an accessor calls its setter with the receiver, or fails without one (sloppy: ignored; strict: TypeError "Cannot set property x of #<Object> which has only a getter"). A primitive base walks its prototype's chain (§6.2.5.6 step 5.a) | `property::set_with_receiver`, `property::set` |
| `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` | ValidateAndApplyPropertyDescriptor for both kinds: steps 5.a-e, then 6.a (data to accessor), 6.b (accessor to data, `writable` false unless given), 6.c (field by field). Data <-> accessor is an attribute change, so a fast object goes to dictionary mode (OM-10) | `property::validate`, `property::define_own_property` |
| Epoch | A new pair in place of an old one bumps the epoch of a prototype, even with the same attributes (an IC may cache a getter found on a prototype while the epoch holds) | `props::own_define` |
| Raw reads | `props::own_get`, `own_entry`, `own_value_or_proto` return the pair of an accessor; a caller tests `attr::ACCESSOR` before it uses the value as a JS value. A pair never reaches JS | `props.rs` |
| Engine text | An error's "Name: message" header (made inside `throw_error`, also on stack overflow) and the reported constructor name read data properties only: no getter runs there | `property::get_data_from` |
| Exotic | An accessor on array `length` is rejected (ArraySetLength step 1). Since M5-P2 ELEM2 an accessor element, an element with other attributes and a non-writable `length` are DICTIONARY elements of the Array (H18 M5-P2 notes); v0 threw "not implemented" for them. String wrapper properties are not configurable, so they reject an accessor | `property::define_own_property` |
| Runtime ids | 12 `DefineAccessorInLiteral` (enumerable) and 16 `DefineClassAccessor` (not enumerable, sets `[[HomeObject]]`): a descriptor with the one present half, DefinePropertyOrThrow; an anonymous function (computed key) is named `"get k"`, `"set k"` or `"get [desc]"` | `interp/slow/runtime.rs` |

## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| Every JS object has a non-zero word0 ShapeId that names a live shape | Heap verifier after every GC (GC stress, test262 `--gc-stress 1`) | M0 wk4 |
| word0 changes only through `shape::install` (`ep9_heap::set_shape`, barrier v2) | Code review; `grep set_shape crates/ep9_vm` | M0 wk4 |
| The same add order from the same root gives the same shape | `two_literals_with_the_same_keys_share_a_shape_id` | M0 wk4 |
| Deleting the last property goes back to the parent shape | `deleting_the_last_property_goes_back_to_the_parent_shape` | M0 wk4 |
| Each OM-10 trigger moves to dictionary mode | `om10_*` tests in `tests/objects.rs` (the index-key trigger as narrowed in M5-P1: `om10_an_index_key_on_a_non_array_makes_a_dictionary` defines string index keys on a plain object with no elements cell; `om10_an_index_the_elements_store_refuses_makes_a_dictionary`: on a plain object with an elements cell a near string index key stays fast, and a far index, as a string or an int32 key, makes a dictionary; after an index key on Object.prototype, a new index [[Set]] on a plain object with no elements cell makes a dictionary, and on one with a cell it stays fast) | M0 wk4 |
| `[[OwnPropertyKeys]]` order: indices, strings in creation order, symbols | `object_keys_and_for_in_order_survive_layout_changes`, `a_lazy_prototype_keeps_its_place_in_the_key_order` | M0 wk4 |
| No content compare of keys in UNIT(ep9_vm) | `EP9_ASSERT_ATOM_KEYS=1 cargo test -p ep9_vm` | M0 wk4 |
| Every shape's proto is null or a prototype-mode object | `debug_assert` in `shape::new_shape`; `set_prototype_of_a_prototype_puts_the_new_prototype_in_prototype_mode` (fast and dictionary objects) | M0 wk4 (P2 fix) |
| A structural change of a prototype bumps the epoch; a value store does not (named and element paths, fast and dictionary prototypes) | `an_object_used_as_a_prototype_gets_a_unique_shape_and_bumps_the_epoch`, `dense_element_changes_of_an_array_prototype_bump_the_epoch` | M0 wk4 (P2 fix) |
| An ACCESSOR_PAIR never changes; a define makes a new one, and a new pair on a prototype bumps the epoch | `validate_and_apply_accessor_descriptors`, `replacing_an_accessor_of_a_prototype_bumps_the_epoch` | M0 wk4 (P3-ACC) |
| Getters and setters get the receiver as `this` (inherited, `super`, primitive bases) | `literal_getters_and_setters_take_the_receiver_as_this`, `keyed_super_getters_and_setters_see_this`, `getters_and_setters_receive_the_receiver_also_a_primitive` | M0 wk4 (P3-ACC) |
| Engine-made error text runs no getter | `engine_error_text_runs_no_getters` | M0 wk4 (P3-ACC) |

## Consequences

- **Gains.** A shape guard is one u32 compare, and the field offset comes from the shape. `shape_of` needs no runtime pointer, so host paths (`proto_of`, `is_extensible`) stay free functions.
- **Costs.** The 64 GiB cage alignment leaves 2,048 cage slots below 2^47. Prototype objects never share shapes. Class constructors with `extends` go to dictionary mode (SetPrototypeOf with own properties).
- **Follow-ups.** P3-IC built the (shape, field) caches (H16 K9-K18). Exotic own properties (array `length` and elements, a constructor's lazy `prototype`, String wrapper properties) are not in shapes: the validity cells cover an element that appears or goes away (an element add or delete on a prototype is a structural change, `shape::note_change`, which clears that prototype's VALIDITY_CELL and the cells below it; M4-P1, H16 K5), but a holder's exotic property is never a field load. P3-ACC built accessors (section above); the super paths pass the receiver (`LoadKeyedFromSuper`, `StoreToSuper`; opcode 0x22 with P3-IC). An IC must not make a data handler for an `ACCESSOR` descriptor: the slot holds the pair. Slack tracking replaces the fixed `new F` capacity of 8.

## Decide before M1a

| Item | Question | v0 today |
|---|---|---|
| Slack tracking | Per-constructor in-object capacity from the first N instances (06 §2.2) | Fixed 4 / n / 8 / 2. **M5-P2 (SLACK):** `new F` gets the count of F's own `this.<name> =` stores, read from F's bytecode, plus the data properties of F.prototype whose value is not callable (at most 8 more), from the first instance on; 8 when the count is unknown (`slack.rs`) |
| Representation and const bits | Field representation (Smi, double, heap) and the constness bit + replacement watchpoints as InvalidatingFuses (H31) | Only attribute bits |
| Elements for every kind | Which kinds have ELEMENTS; index keys on non-arrays in elements, not dictionary mode (H18) | Arrays only; index keys -> dictionary. **M5-P1 (ELEM1):** the kinds in `elements::plain_elements_kind` (OBJECT, FUNCTION, ERROR, ITERATOR, BOUND_FUNCTION, GENERATOR, SLOT_OBJECT) keep {w, e, c} index keys at +16 (H18 M5-P1 notes); Array keeps its own paths and ARGUMENTS its own +16 elements; a primitive wrapper (PRIMITIVE_WRAPPER: Number, Boolean, Symbol, BigInt and String wrappers) has no +16 store and keeps an index key as a stored property (dictionary; a String wrapper's own string indices stay exotic); module namespaces and proxies have no +16 store; a string index key on an object with no elements cell still -> dictionary |
| Transition hashing | Transition table form past one child; the 64-child cap | Linear table, cap 64 |
| Validity cells | Prototype-chain validity cells per PROTO_INFO in place of one global epoch. **Decided and built 2026-10-26 (M4-P1 VALIDITY, V9-D7; H16 K5):** V8 style: one VALIDITY_CELL (79, 48 B: +8 int32 state, +16 a weak users list, +24 int32 users, +32 int32 depth, +40 int32 the epoch when made) per prototype in its PROTO_INFO +32; a structural change of a prototype clears its cell and the cells of every prototype below it; chains deeper than 64 get no cell. Compiled code (JIT and unit code) depends on prototype-chain validity through a Dynamic FuseRef of the descriptor kind "prototype-chain validity by ProtoRef" (H31-Q1), never on a VALIDITY_CELL itself: an invalid cell stays invalid, and the next IC miss makes a new one | Validity cells (`validity.rs`); `proto_epoch` still counts, for a debug cross-check only ([2026-11-01] it counts after M5-P0 too: M5-P0 removed only the `Cx` pointer to the epoch (`Cx::proto_epoch`) and the epoch argument of the IC hit paths, 89a6bf9b; a new cell keeps the epoch at +40, and the debug cross-check in `ic.rs` reads `ShapeState::proto_epoch` itself) |
| S2 weak transition child | Reading a weak transition child is safe because marking is stop-the-world. With incremental marking, a weak child that is read and stored must be marked by the barrier or the final rescan | Stop-the-world marking |
| Dictionary mode | Shared dictionary shapes per (prototype, kind, extensible): is a per-object shape needed for IC misses? The PROPS table is scanned linearly: when does it get a hash index? | Shared shapes; linear scan |
| Accessor ICs | Getter/setter call handlers (V8 LoadHandler for accessors, JSC GetterSetter cached by identity): cache the pair or the getter, under the epoch or a per-holder validity cell? Data <-> accessor goes to dictionary mode today; does a fast object need an accessor transition instead? | Accessors take the IC slow path; any data <-> accessor change goes to dictionary mode |
| Element accessors | Accessor elements (and non-default element attributes) need a sparse or dictionary elements kind (H18) | "not implemented" for Arrays. **M5-P1 (ELEM1):** on other objects such an element moves to the property storage (a stored index key, dictionary mode), no throw. **M5-P2 (ELEM2):** on an Array it is a DICTIONARY entry (value or ACCESSOR_PAIR, attributes), and a non-writable `length` is a flag of the ELEMENTS_DICT cell; the throw is gone |

Proposals for the rows above (Draft, 2026-10-27, M4-P2 ADR-A0; plan v9 §4.1 tasks 5 and 9). Each is built and measured before M1a unless it says so; the "v0 today" column stays the as-built state.

| Item | Proposal |
|---|---|
| Slack tracking | V8 style, per root shape of a prototype (the PROTO_INFO root shape that `new F` instances start from; V9-D8 keeps the initial shape in the Construct site's Call slot). The first N instances (N = 7, est.; V8 starts its counter at 7, external) get a generous capacity: 8, or the count of `this.<name> =` stores in the constructor's bytecode + 8, at most 255 (word0 flags bits 0-7). Then the root shape records the largest field count seen, and later instances get that capacity. A live object never shrinks (H21 P5): the first N keep their slack. Built in month 5 (SLACK). **As built (M5-P2 SLACK; replaces the run-time tracker above):** the capacity is read from the constructor's bytecode at the first construct, so every instance of `new F` gets it and starts from one root shape. `slack::instance_capacity` (called by `call::ordinary_create_from_constructor`) counts F's distinct `this.<name> =` stores (`SetNamedProperty` and `DefineNamedOwnProperty` on a register that holds `this`), plus the own data properties of F.prototype whose value is not callable and that F does not store (at most 8; `F.prototype.left = null` makes room for the instances' own `left`), at most 255. The count is unknown, and the capacity stays 8, when `this` may leave the frame (a call or a store that reads it, a closure or context slot, a direct eval), for a keyed store on `this`, a derived constructor, no store, or more than 64 names. A field added after the constructor goes out of line, as a ninth field did. The count is a size hint, never a correctness input. The capacity is chosen at F's first construct, from F's code and the prototype F has then, and F's FunctionInfo keeps it (`slack_capacity`, one byte, no Value), so a later construct runs no scan (a scan at each cold construct cost about 8,300 instructions) and a prototype that gains a data field later changes no capacity (M5-P2 integration fixer; it replaced a per-thread cache keyed by a hash of the code bytes, which could hand one function another function's constant indices). Why not the tracker: a construct site that switched to a final root after N cold constructs (built and measured in M5-P2) made two root shape families per constructor, and the T0 ICs saw both: richards +12.7%, deltablue +9.0%, raytrace +2.4%, splay +1.5% instructions, earley-boyer only -2.8% (mac a64, `EP9_HASH_SEED=1`). A shape tree cannot shrink in place here (H21 P5; V8 shrinks its maps). The construct site keeps the root shape of its first instance (`construct_ic.rs`), which already has the final capacity. The root shape `flags` bits 4-7 (`shape::flag::SLACK_SHIFT`, OBJECT_LAYOUT 5) are not used: no counter is needed. It reads no execution policy (rule 40; `tests::slack` checks equal capacities with and without feedback vectors) |
| Representation and const bits | Per field descriptor: a representation {none, int32, double, heap object, any}, one-way (none -> int32 -> double -> any; none -> heap object -> any), and a const bit that a second store after the first initializing store clears. A Value already holds a double unboxed (H6), so double needs no box; the representation is a fact for field loads (H30), not a storage form. A generalization or a const clear changes the descriptor in place and pops the field's fuse. The constness bit and the replacement watchpoints register as InvalidatingFuses named by a Dynamic FuseRef descriptor (H31-Q1), read through a LinkSlot; no code assumes a fuse-word address. Built in month 5 (CONST/REP), on the H31 registry v0. **As built (2026-10-29, M5-P2 CONSTREP; C-02):** details bits 4-6 hold the representation (`shape::rep`: NONE 0, INT32 1, DOUBLE 2, HEAP 3 = no number (a cell, undefined, null, a boolean), ANY 4; join: NONE gives the other, INT32 and DOUBLE give DOUBLE, else ANY), bit 7 the const bit. The first store of a field is its add (`props::own_add`): the new descriptor gets the value's representation and const; an add along a transition that other objects took widens the field to the value and keeps const. Every later store (`props::field_set`) is a second store: it widens and clears const for the whole family, so const means no object of the family replaced the value (V8's const field). A family is the owner shape that added the field (`shape::field_owner`) and its live descendants; a branch holds a copy of the entry, and a widening writes every copy in place (`shape::set_field_bits_in_family`); a prototype-mode object leaves its tree family with its own copy of the cell (`shape::proto_info_for` gives the new unique shape a copy of a tree shape's DESCRIPTORS, `shape::own_descriptors`; M5-P2 integration fixer), so no cell is shared between families and a widening never writes a cell that the family's owner, and so its kind-3 fuse, does not read. An elements-kind transition joins the old shape's field bits into the new one (`shape::with_elements_kind`). A shape made with no value known (`shape::add`: the cached function shapes) holds ANY, not const. A widening pops the field's Dynamic fuse, descriptor kind 3 "field constness and representation" (H31 As built (9)). Where its word is reached (M5-P1-ORCH-13 seam, before M1a): no new word; a compiled kind-3 GuardFuse read loads the field's details through the shape (SHAPE +24 DESCRIPTORS, the entry's details word) and compares bits 4-7 with the bits it was compiled for, which only widen; the registry's Rust word mirrors it. Store ICs (H16 handler kinds, appended): a field case (0x24, 0x25) of a field that is not ANY records a checked smi kind (9-14: INT32, NUMBER or HEAP, in-object odd, out of line even), and its hit stores only a value whose tag fits (else a miss, which widens); an ADD hit stores only a value that the target's new field holds; a global store case widens its binding to ANY, not const, because its hit checks no value. Direct field writers outside these paths must store only values the field's bits allow or widen first (`field_rep::widen`, `field_rep::widen_all`); CloneObject's fast path (`literal_site.rs` `fast_clone`) copies a source's fields into the result with no value check, so the cold path that records a case widens every field of the result shape to ANY, not const (`literal_site::miss_clone`, `field_rep::widen_all`; M5-P2 integration fixer). A representation is a fact for compiled loads (H30), not a storage form: no field is boxed or moved. T0 cost (V10-D16): the checked store hits and the ADD check (Review record row 4 of the 2026-10-29 fill row) |
| Elements for every kind | Every JS object kind may hold elements in its +16 ELEMENTS slot (H18-Q3); an array-index key on a non-array goes there, so it is no longer an OM-10 trigger. The elements kind lives in the shape, so one shape compare guards the kind too. String wrappers, ARGUMENTS and module namespaces keep their exotic hooks. Built in month 5 (ELEMENTS-KINDS, V9-D39). **As built (M5-P1 ELEM1):** so, with one change: a string index key goes to +16 only once the object has an elements cell (an int32 key makes it); before that it is still an OM-10 trigger, because the index test of a string key is a call that every named define would pay (BENCH-SET ci-v0; H18-Q3) |
| Transition hashing | The first child stays in the weak +32 word, and up to 8 more stay in the linear TRANSITIONS table, as today (`shape.rs` module docs). Above 8 (the threshold of the descriptor hash index), the TRANSITIONS cell becomes a hashed table keyed by (key, attributes), with weak entries as now. The live-transition cap goes from 64 to 1,024 (est.); past it the object goes to dictionary mode as today. Measure the miss path on the BENCH-SET before the cap is fixed |
| S2 weak transition child | No weak read barrier (H21 P6): the shape store of a transition goes through `set_shape`, whose slow path re-greys a BLACK host; the final stop-the-world fixpoint rescans every root (CSS included) before weak transition slots are cleared (03 §4 S2b). So a child that is read and installed during marking stays alive. Checked by V2 and V3 after each S2b cycle (M5) |
| Dictionary mode | Dictionary v1: keep the shared dictionary shapes per (prototype, kind, extensible); an IC case on a dictionary receiver stays `SLOW` (H16 K12). Add a hash index to the PROPS table above 8 entries (the descriptor threshold). A dictionary-mode holder on the prototype chain gets a handler that looks the key up in the holder's PROPS at each hit, under the receiver's validity cell (V8 LoadNormal style), so a prototype holder with many properties still hits. Built in month 6 (DICT v1) |
| Accessor ICs | Getter and setter handlers: new IC_HANDLER kinds (appended after CLONE 7, H16 handler kinds), +32 the field location in both forms. **Own accessor** (the receiver has the property): +16 int32 0, +24 undefined (no holder); the hit loads the ACCESSOR_PAIR from the **current receiver** at the location. A shape does not name one object: two objects of one shape can hold different pairs, because `shape::add` reuses a transition by key and attributes and `props::own_add` stores each object's pair in that object's own field. So an own case never caches a holder. **Inherited accessor** (a prototype on the chain has it): +16 the validity cell of the chain, +24 the holder (weak); the hit loads the pair from the cached holder, which the chain validity guards. Either hit then calls the pair's getter or setter. A pair is immutable, so a redefine makes a new pair that the next hit reads; no pair identity is cached in T0 (JIT code may guard on it). Data <-> accessor stays a dictionary move in v1; an accessor transition only if a benchmark needs it. Built in month 6 (ACCESSOR ICs) |
| Element accessors | Accessor elements and non-default element attributes move the object to the DICTIONARY elements kind (H18-Q2), whose entries hold (value or ACCESSOR_PAIR, attributes). Dense kinds keep {w, e, c} only. This removes the "not implemented" throw. Built with ELEMENTS-KINDS (month 5). **M5-P1 (ELEM1), before DICTIONARY:** on an object that is not an Array, such an element leaves the elements store for the property storage (a stored index key; dictionary mode, OM-10), so no throw; Arrays keep the throw until M5-P2 ELEM2. **Built for Arrays (M5-P2 ELEM2):** as proposed (H18-Q2 built); other objects keep the M5-P1 rule |

**Decided (2026-11-01, M5-P4 FREEZE-PREP; H17-Q1).** Each row of the two tables above is decided as below. The "v0 today" and Proposal cells keep their text (doc 13 and H31 cite them by line). The last column names the Freeze-table part that holds the row.

| Item | Decided | Part |
|---|---|---|
| Slack tracking | As built (M5-P2 SLACK; the As-built text of its Proposals row). Where the capacity lives is layout: word0 flags bits 0-7 and shape `counts` bits 12-19 hold it (at most 255, `obj::MAX_INOBJECT`), F's FunctionInfo keeps it in one byte (`slack_capacity`), and the root shape `flags` bits 4-7 are 0 today (a new flag there is an append, "representation values (append-only)"). The numbers that choose it are tuning values, and the capacity is a fact of the run: it reads F.prototype at F's first construct, so compiled code reads it from the run and never bakes it (MODES-CHECK-w20 MC-w20-12) | layout core (the form); layout tuning (the numbers) |
| Representation and const bits | As built (M5-P2 CONSTREP; C-02). The bit positions are layout: details bits 4-6 hold the representation and bit 7 the const bit (`shape::DETAILS_CONST`). The representation values as built (NONE 0, INT32 1, DOUBLE 2, HEAP 3, ANY 4; `shape::rep`) never change, and a new value (5-7) is an append (M5-P5-ORCH-2). The write rules get a keep-or-drop test at A4 (M5-P3-ORCH-3; doc 13 R13), so they are a gated part | layout core (the bit positions); representation values (the values); rep-const write rules (the rules) |
| Elements for every kind | As built (M5-P1 ELEM1; H18-Q3) | layout core |
| Transition hashing | The Proposal. Built in month 6 | Transition table form |
| Validity cells | As built (M4-P1 VALIDITY) | layout core |
| S2 weak transition child | The Proposal: no weak read barrier; the final stop-the-world rescan keeps a child that is read and installed during marking. Checked by V2 and V3 after each S2b cycle | S2 weak transition child |
| Dictionary mode | As built today; the Proposal (Dictionary v1) is built in month 6 | layout core (the OM-10 triggers as built); Dictionary v1 (the v1 form) |
| Accessor ICs | The Proposal. Built in month 6 | Accessor ICs |
| Element accessors | As built (M5-P1 ELEM1 for other objects; M5-P2 ELEM2 for Arrays) | layout core |

The Decision row "Index keys on prototypes" is its own part too: the sticky `shape::proto_index_keys` flag is built, and month 6 replaces it with the `proto_index` "no-elements prototypes" fuse (H31-Q5; plan v10 V10-D13), so it freezes at M1 exit, not at M1a.

## Freeze table (README §3.4)

H17 freezes by part (plan v10 V10-D9, V10-A22; README §3.4). At M1a the Frozen step writes `Frozen (layout core; <date>, M1a)`. The parts due at M1 exit join that list when they freeze. A part name holds no `,` and no ` and `, because the Frozen check splits the Status parenthesis there (`tools/xtask/src/exit/freeze.rs:113-125`).

| Part | What it holds | Freeze point | After the freeze point | Check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| layout core | Every built row of this ADR, except what the parts below hold: the word0 layout (shape id, the kind byte, the flags bits; the `kinds.rs` module doc); the JSObject header (+8 SLOTS or PROPS, +16 ELEMENTS, +24 the fixed fields, then the in-object slots); the fixed fields of each kind and the internal cell layouts (the Decision tables); the layouts of the kinds as built only, ids 1-5, 8, 16-28, 64-82 and 128 (0 is refused; `crates/ep9_vm/src/kinds.rs:72-152`); the declared kinds that are not built are owned by their own parts, not by this one: EXT_LATIN1 6 and EXT_UTF16 7 by the H19 part "external strings" (M4 exit), FUSE 83 by its own build (H31-Q1, only if a fuse word needs a cell); the +16 elements slot and the kinds that keep a store there (`elements::plain_elements_kind`, ARRAY, ARGUMENTS); the shape cell (64 B) with its `counts` bits (0-11 property count, 12-19 capacity, 20-27 kind, 28-31 elements kind) and `flags` bits (bits 4-7 are always 0 today; a new flag there is an append, in "representation values (append-only)"); the details word (attributes in bits 0-3; the representation bits 4-6 and the const bit 7, their positions only; the representation values are in "representation values (append-only)"); the shape-of rule with the 64 GiB cage alignment (H17-Q2); the field-index rule; prototype mode and PROTO_INFO; the validity cells; the epoch rule; dictionary mode as built (the OM-10 triggers; their numbers are in layout tuning); the accessor pair; keys; the function `length` and `name` slots; the slack form (where the capacity lives); the native-word form: every native word in a cell is `address + NATIVE_WORD_OFFSET` (one bit, so native code decodes it with one `eor`; `crates/ep9_vm/src/layout.rs:230-233`; H25-Q1), a fact of Value encoding 1 (`ep9_value::encoding::VERSION`), not of OBJECT_LAYOUT 5 (`crates/ep9_vm/src/layout.rs:230`); and every Rules row | M1a | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | OBJECT_LAYOUT 5 owns these facts (`crates/ep9_vm/src/versions.rs:41`), except the native-word form, a fact of Value encoding 1; whether a D6 switch moves a version is B3's (plan-v11-inputs item 30). Unit-header item (M5-P0-ORCH-6): the H35 header holds `object_layout` (`crates/ep9_unit/src/header.rs:108`) but no VM_LAYOUT field, and VM_LAYOUT owns the `Cx`, FunctionInfo and RuntimeTable offsets that native code bakes (`crates/ep9_vm/src/versions.rs:60-69`): H35 (C-18) adds the field before a unit holds native code (A2) |

A new kind takes a new id in its range; no id is renumbered or reused; a retired id stays reserved; 255 is never assigned; the routed range 25..=28 is closed (the 2026-10-29 C-24 note in the Decision) | None | An append is a fill (README §3.4 "Append-only parts"); an existing id never changes; G8 never counts an append | `tests::layout3::kind_ids_are_an_append_only_byte_list`, `kinds::tests::table_v1_ranges` |

The shape `flags` bits 4-7: always 0 today (`shape::flag::SLACK_MASK`, unused since M5-P2 SLACK); a new flag there is next, by append. In every build a field of an unknown rep takes no value without a widening (`field_rep::fits`), and a store IC gets no checked handler for it (`ic::smi::checked`). The positions stay in the layout core; the join and store rules stay in "rep-const write rules (gated)" | None | An append is a fill (README §3.4 "Append-only parts"); an existing value never changes; G8 never counts an append | `tests::layout5::the_shape_bit_positions` (the values as built); `tests::layout5::h17_rep_value_probe_7` (rep 7, the top of the 3-bit field, never the next free value 5) |

| layout tuning (gated) | The T0 tuning numbers of the object layout: the in-object capacity of `{}` (4) and of an object with no count (8, `slack::GENEROUS`); the slack scan limits (64 names, `slack::MAX_NAMES`; 64 tracked registers; at most 8 prototype fields); the OM-10 numbers (1020 properties, `shape::MAX_FAST_PROPERTIES`; 64 live transitions, `shape::MAX_TRANSITIONS`; a descriptor hash index above 8 keys, `shape::LINEAR_MAX`); the SLOTS growth max(need, 2 x old, 4); the plain elements gap 1024 (`elements::PLAIN_GAP`). Not a part: "Spike C read set" (V10-D9 adds it only if the SPIKEC-6/7 adoption missed M1a). It did not miss: SPIKEC-7 proved the Spike C layout reads again on live objects after ELEM2 (`vm_layout::check_elem2`, M5-P3), and its 4 hand facts (CAGE_ALIGN, the shape `counts` word at +48, SHAPE 128, CONTEXT 66) are layout-core facts; moving them into the `layout` export (B3, owner OBJECT_LAYOUT 5) changes no layout.

## Validation

- UNIT(ep9_vm) with GC stress 1 and the heap verifier: `tests/objects.rs`, `tests/shapes.rs`.
- test262 exec and exec `--gc-stress 1`: 0 NEW FAILURE.
- M1a: the SP-Facts and IC work check the guard + load path (H16).

## Open questions

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H17-Q1 | Every row of "Decide before M1a" | M1a | v0 as built. **Decided (2026-11-01, M5-P4 FREEZE-PREP):** the "Decided" table under "Decide before M1a": the built rows as built, in the layout core; the rows built in month 6 as proposed, each in a part with its own freeze point (the Freeze table) |
| H17-Q2 | Is 64 GiB cage alignment kept, or does `shape_of` read the cage base from the runtime? | M1a | Keep: it saves a load in every host path. **Decided (2026-11-01, M5-P4 FREEZE-PREP): keep.** `shape_of` masks the address with `shape::CAGE_ALIGN` (64 GiB) and needs no runtime pointer; Spike C bakes the same rule (its hand fact `SHAPE_CAGE_ALIGN`, proved by `vm_layout::check`). In the layout core |

## Reopens if

- An IC or JIT measurement shows the shared dictionary shapes or prototype mode cost more than the plan's budget.
- The cage must be larger than 64 GiB.

(1) The code matches: `literal_site::miss_clone` calls `field_rep::widen_all(rt, rs)` on the result shape before it records or updates a case, and `widen_all` widens each field of a fast shape to ANY, not const, for the field's family (`field_rep::widen`); a dictionary shape never gets a case (`clonable`). `fast_clone` hits only on a recorded case, so a hit copies into fields that allow any value. The new test `a_clone_hit_stores_only_into_fields_that_allow_any_value` passes, and so does Codex R1's own test for this defect (`verify_clone_hit_breaks_the_field_rep`, added for the run and removed): the result field is rep 4 = ANY after the hit. The As-built cell names the writer, and `widen_all` loses its `dead_code` allow. (2) Kind test applied (README §3.3 fill: 'Adds a rule or value where the ADR had an Open question or a part the plan defers. Every existing Rules check, test and golden file keeps its result'): it builds the part the CONSTREP fill row left open ('Open: CloneObject's fast path copies fields with no widening'); the other `tests::literal_site` and `tests::field_rep` tests keep their results -> fill. Author: fill (H17 is Draft). Confirmed. Open (c): `widen_all` clears const and the representation of the whole result family at the first recorded case (with an `Object.prototype` source, the source's own family), so a month-6 reader loses those facts; Codex's per-hit form (`field_rep::on_init_store` per copied field) would keep them.
